name: Docker Build & Push

on:
  push:
    branches: [main]
    paths:
      - 'Dockerfile'
      - 'ai-worker/Dockerfile'
      - 'docker-compose.yml'
      - '.github/workflows/docker.yml'
  pull_request:
    branches: [main]
    paths:
      - 'Dockerfile'
      - 'ai-worker/Dockerfile'
      - 'docker-compose.yml'
      - '.github/workflows/docker.yml'

permissions:
  contents: read
  packages: write

jobs:
  build-go-api:
    name: Build go-api
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v6

      - uses: docker/setup-qemu-action@v4

      - uses: docker/setup-buildx-action@v4

      - uses: docker/login-action@v4
        if: github.event_name == 'push' && github.ref == 'refs/heads/main'
        with:
          registry: ghcr.io
          username: ${{ github.actor }}
          password: ${{ secrets.GITHUB_TOKEN }}

      - uses: docker/build-push-action@v7
        with:
          context: .
          file: Dockerfile
          push: ${{ github.event_name == 'push' && github.ref == 'refs/heads/main' }}
          platforms: linux/amd64,linux/arm64
          cache-from: type=gha
          cache-to: type=gha,mode=max
          tags: |
            ghcr.io/${{ github.repository_owner }}/go-api:${{ github.sha }}
            ghcr.io/${{ github.repository_owner }}/go-api:latest

  build-python-worker:
    name: Build python-worker
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v6

      - uses: docker/setup-qemu-action@v4

      - uses: docker/setup-buildx-action@v4

      - uses: docker/login-action@v4
        if: github.event_name == 'push' && github.ref == 'refs/heads/main'
        with:
          registry: ghcr.io
          username: ${{ github.actor }}
          password: ${{ secrets.GITHUB_TOKEN }}

      - uses: docker/build-push-action@v7
        with:
          context: ai-worker
          file: ai-worker/Dockerfile
          push: ${{ github.event_name == 'push' && github.ref == 'refs/heads/main' }}
          platforms: linux/amd64,linux/arm64
          cache-from: type=gha
          cache-to: type=gha,mode=max
          tags: |
            ghcr.io/${{ github.repository_owner }}/python-worker:${{ github.sha }}
            ghcr.io/${{ github.repository_owner }}/python-worker:latest
name: Frontend CI

on:
  push:
    branches: [main]
    paths:
      - 'frontend/**'
  pull_request:
    branches: [

### https://docs.github.com/en/repositories/managing-your-repositorys-settings-and-features/customizing-your-repository/about-code-owners > Checklist (3)
- uses: actions/setup-node@v6
        with:
          node-version: '22'
          cache: npm
          cache-dependency-path: frontend/package-lock.json

      - run: npm ci

      - run: npm run build

      - run: npx playwright install --with-deps chromium

      - run: npm run test:e2e
name: Go CI

on:
  push:
    branches: [main]
    paths:
      - 'cmd/**'
      - 'internal/**'
      - 'pkg/**'
      - 'go.mod'
      - 'go.sum'
  pull_request:
    branches: [main]
    paths:
      - 'cmd/**'
      - 'internal/**'
      - 'pkg/**'
      - 'go.mod'
      - 'go.sum'

permissions:
  contents: read

jobs:
  build-and-test:
    name: Build & Test
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v6

      - uses: actions/setup-go@v6
        with:
          go-version: '1.26'
          cache: true

      - run: go mod download

      - run: go build ./cmd/api

      - run: go test -race -coverprofile=coverage.txt ./...

      - run: go test -race -run '^TestMigrationsUpAndDown$' ./migrations/...

      - uses: codecov/codecov-action@v6
        with:
          files: coverage.txt
          fail_ci_if_error: false

      - run: go vet ./...

  lint:
    name: Lint
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v6

      - uses: actions/setup-go@v6

### https://docs.github.com/en/repositories/managing-your-repositorys-settings-and-features/customizing-your-repository/about-code-owners
# https://docs.github.com/en/repositories/managing-your-repositorys-settings-and-features/customizing-your-repository/about-code-owners

* @jobinlawrance
version: 2

updates:
  # Go modules (root)
  - package-ecosystem: "gomod"
    directory: "/"
    schedule:
      interval: "weekly"
    labels:
      - "dependencies"
      - "go"
    commit-message:
      prefix: "deps(go):"
    open-pull-requests-limit: 10

  # Python / pip (ai-worker)
  - package-ecosystem: "pip"
    directory: "/ai-worker"
    schedule:
      interval: "weekly"
    labels:
      - "dependencies"
      - "python"
    commit-message:
      prefix: "deps(py):"
    open-pull-requests-limit: 10

  # npm (frontend)
  - package-ecosystem: "npm"
    directory: "/frontend"
    schedule:
      interval: "weekly"
    labels:
      - "dependencies"
      - "javascript"
    commit-message:
      prefix: "deps(npm):"
    open-pull-requests-limit: 10

  # Docker (root)
  - package-ecosystem: "docker"
    directory: "/"
    schedule:
      interval: "weekly"
    labels:
      - "dependencies"
      - "docker"
    commit-message:
      prefix: "deps(docker):"
    open-pull-requests-limit: 10

  # Docker (ai-worker)
  - package-ecosystem: "docker"
    directory: "/ai-worker"
    schedule:
      interval: "weekly"
    labels:
      - "dependencies"
      - "docker"
    commit-message:
      prefix: "deps(docker):"
    open-pull-requests-limit: 10

  # GitHub Actions
  - package-ecosystem: "github-actions"
    directory: "/"
    schedule:
      interval: "weekly"
    labels:
      - "dependencies"
      - "ci"
    commit-message:
      prefix: "deps(actions):"
    open-pull-requests-limit: 10

### README > Tech Stack
## Tech Stack

| Layer | Technology | Purpose |
|-------|-----------|---------|
| **API Server** | Go + Gin | REST API, JWT validation, tenant routing, SSE streaming |
| **AI Worker** | Python + gRPC | RAG queries, embeddings, document parsing, web scraping |
| **Database** | PostgreSQL 18 + pgvector | Relational data, vector search, BM25 full-text |
| **Frontend** | Vue.js 3 + Tailwind CSS | Admin dashboard (SPA, mobile-first, PWA-capable) |
| **Chatbot Widget** | Web Component | Embeddable `<raven-chat>` element |
| **Auth** | Keycloak | OIDC/OAuth2, user management, multi-tenant realms |
| **Job Queue** | Valkey (Redis fork) | Async document processing, caching, rate limiting |
| **Object Storage** | SeaweedFS | S3-compatible file storage (Apache 2.0) |
| **Voice** | LiveKit Server + Agents | WebRTC SFU, STT/LLM/TTS voice pipeline |
| **Reverse Proxy** | Traefik | Auto-TLS, routing, security headers |

### README > Architecture Overview
## Architecture Overview

Raven uses a **two-process architecture**: a Go API server handles HTTP routing, authentication, and orchestration, while a Python AI worker handles all ML/AI workloads (embedding, RAG queries, document parsing, web scraping). The two communicate over gRPC, with Valkey (Redis-compatible) as the async job queue.

PostgreSQL serves as the single source of truth -- storing relational data, vector embeddings (pgvector), and full-text search indexes. A Vue.js SPA provides the admin dashboard, and Keycloak handles identity management.

## docker containers services

### Usage: docker compose -f docker-compose.admin.yml up -d
# Usage: docker compose -f docker-compose.admin.yml up -d

services:
  # ─── Beszel Hub (server monitoring dashboard) ──────────────────────────────
  beszel-hub:
    image: henrygd/beszel:0.18.7
    container_name: beszel-hub
    # Host networking required: the Beszel agent communicates via SSH on localhost:45876,
    # so the hub must share the host network to reach it directly.
    network_mode: host
    environment:
      USER_CREATION: "true"
    volumes:
      - beszel-data:/beszel_data
    restart: unless-stopped

  # ─── Beszel Agent ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  # The agent is started as a standalone docker container by Ansible,
  # NOT via this compose file. See roles/admin-tools/tasks/main.yml.

  # ─── Dockhand (Docker management UI) ──────────────────────────────────────
  # Docker socket is mounted read-only. For production hardening, consider
  # replacing with a socket proxy (e.g. tecnativa/docker-socket-proxy).
  dockhand:
    image: fnsys/dockhand:latest
    container_name: dockhand
    ports:
      - "127.0.0.1:3000:3000"
    volumes:
      - /var/run/docker.sock:/var/run/docker.sock:ro
      - dockhand-data:/app/data
    restart: unless-stopped

  # ─── Glance (self-hosted dashboard) ────────────────────────────────────────
  glance:
    image: glanceapp/glance:0.8.4
    container_name: glance
    ports:
      - "127.0.0.1:8085:8080"
    volumes:
      - ./glance/config:/app/config
    dns:
      - 8.8.8.8
      - 1.1.1.1
    restart: unless-stopped

volumes:
  beszel-data:
  dockhand-data:

# eBPF Edge Optimization — Design Spec

**Date:** 2026-04-07
**Issues:** #120 (XDP Pre-filtering), #122 (Kernel Observability), #123 (Security Audit Trail)
**Milestone:** Edge Optimization (M10)
**Tier:** Enterprise

---

## Overview

Three eBPF-based features built on a shared Go foundation inside the Raven API binary. No separate monitoring agents, no extra processes on edge nodes. All features degrade gracefully to no-op when eBPF is unavailable (missing capabilities, non-Linux, kernel too old).

**Implementation order:**
1. Shared foundation (`internal/ebpf/`)
2. #122 Kernel Observability — validates foundation, lowest risk
3. #123 Security Audit Trail — ring buffer pattern, builds on #122
4. #120 XDP Pre-filtering — most complex, isolated blast radius

---

## Architecture

### Shared Foundation: `internal/ebpf/`

One package owns the eBPF lifecycle. All three features depend on it.

**Files:**

| File | Responsibility |
|------|---------------|
| `capabilities.go` | Check `CAP_BPF` / `CAP_NET_ADMIN` at startup; detect kernel version (floor: 5.8); return typed error if missing; calls `setrlimit(RLIMIT_MEMLOCK)` on kernels < 5.11 |
| `loader.go` | Wrap `cilium/ebpf` collection loading; detect BTF availability; return typed handles |
| `manager.go` | Lifecycle: `Start()` / `Stop()` / `io.Closer`; detach all probes on shutdown; hooked into API server SIGTERM |
| `maps.go` | Typed helpers for BPF hash maps and ring buffers shared between kernel and userspace |
| `programs/` | All `.c` BPF programs; compiled by `bpf2go` at build time into embedded `_bpfel.go`/`_bpfeb.go` |

**Key invariant:** If eBPF is unavailable at runtime, all features self-disable with a structured `slog` warning. The API server starts and serves normally.

**Library:** `github.com/cilium/ebpf` (Cilium's ebpf-go). BPF C programs compiled via `bpf2go` — bytecode embedded into the Go binary at build time. No runtime C toolchain required on edge nodes.

---

## Feature #122: Kernel-level Observability

**Package:** `internal/ebpf/observability/`
**Config:** `RAVEN_EBPF_OBSERVABILITY_ENABLED=true`

### BPF Programs (`programs/observability.c`)

| Program type | Hook | Metric |
|---|---|---|
| `tp_btf` | `sched_switch` | Per-process CPU time (via `prev_sum_exec_runtime` delta) |
| `tracepoint` | `raw_syscalls/sys_exit` | Syscall error rates by syscall nr (all syscalls, single tracepoint) |
| `tracepoint` | `net/net_dev_start_xmit` + `net/netif_receive_skb` | Network bytes in/out per PID |
| `kprobe` | `__fd_install` | File descriptor count (kernel-internal symbol; monitor for rename across kernel upgrades) |

### Userspace (`observability/collector.go`)

- Polls BPF maps on configurable interval (default 15s)
- Converts raw BPF counters → OTel `Gauge`/`Counter` instruments
- Registers with existing `MeterProvider` from `internal/telemetry/`
- `InitProvider()` in `internal/telemetry/telemetry.go` optionally initialises collector after OTel is ready

**New OTel metrics exported:**

| Metric | Type | Unit |
|--------|------|------|
| `ebpf.process.cpu_time` | Counter | milliseconds |
| `ebpf.net.bytes_in` | Counter | bytes |
| `ebpf.net.bytes_out` | Counter | bytes |
| `ebpf.syscall.errors` | Counter | count |
| `ebpf.fd.count` | Gauge | count |

**Replaces:** Prometheus node exporter on edge nodes — zero additional process on Pi.

---

## Feature #123: Security Audit Trail

**Package:** `internal/ebpf/audit/`
**Config:** `RAVEN_EBPF_AUDIT_ENABLED=true`

### BPF Programs (`programs/audit.c`)

| Program type | Hook | Event |
|---|---|---|
| `tracepoint` | `syscalls/sys_enter_execve` | Process spawn: PID, binary path, parent PID, timestamp |
| `tracepoint` | `sock/inet_sock_set_state` | TCP connection established: src/dst IP, port |
| `tracepoint` | `syscalls/sys_enter_connect` | Outbound connect: destination IP + port |

Events written to a **BPF ring buffer** — efficient, ordered, no polling overhead.

**Ring buffer sizing:** `RAVEN_EBPF_AUDIT_RING_BUFFER_SIZE` (default `1048576` = 1MB, must be a power of 2). On overflow, the kernel drops events and increments a lost-event counter.

### Userspace (`audit/consumer.go`)

- Reads ring buffer in dedicated goroutine via `ringbuf.Reader`
- On `ringbuf.ErrRingbufferFull`: increments `ebpf.audit.dropped_events` OTel counter (Counter, unit: count) and logs a warning — no panic, no crash
- Emits structured `slog` JSON log entries into existing logging pipeline (same OTLP endpoint)
- Configurable IP allowlist in a BPF hash map — alerts on connections outside the list
- Configurable exec path allowlist — alerts on unexpected binary spawns
- All alerts tagged with `audit.violation=true` for downstream SIEM filtering

**Config vars:**

| Var | Default | Purpose |
|-----|---------|---------|
| `RAVEN_EBPF_AUDIT_IP_ALLOWLIST` | `""` | Comma-separated CIDRs allowed for outbound |
| `RAVEN_EBPF_AUDIT_EXEC_ALLOWLIST` | `""` | Comma-separated binary paths allowed to exec |
| `RAVEN_EBPF_AUDIT_RING_BUFFER_SIZE` | `1048576` | Ring buffer size in bytes (power of 2) |

**New OTel metrics exported (Feature #123):**

| Metric | Type | Unit |
|--------|------|------|
| `ebpf.audit.dropped_events` | Counter | count |

**Power-of-2 validation:** `internal/config/config.go` validates `AuditRingBufferSize` on load using `bits.OnesCount(uint(size)) == 1 && size > 0`. A non-power-of-2 value returns a clear config error before any BPF load is attempted, preventing the opaque kernel error from `ringbuf.NewReader`.

**Compliance target:** GDPR/SOC2 audit log of all process activity and network connections on edge nodes.

---

## Feature #120: XDP Pre-filtering

**Package:** `internal/ebpf/xdp/`
**Config:** `RAVEN_EBPF_XDP_ENABLED=true`, `RAVEN_EBPF_XDP_INTERFACE=eth0`

### BPF Program (`programs/xdp.c`)

```
XDP hook on primary NIC
→ Parse Ethernet/IP header
→ Lookup src IP in blocked_ips BPF hash map
   → BLOCKED:   XDP_DROP  (never reaches TCP stack)
   → THROTTLED: XDP_DROP if rate exceeded, else XDP_PASS
   → DEFAULT:   XDP_PASS
```

**Two BPF maps:**

| Map | Type | Content |
|-----|------|---------|
| `blocked_ips` | `BPF_MAP_TYPE_LPM_TRIE` | Permanently blocked CIDRs; uses LPM for prefix matching. Go-side insert uses `struct bpf_lpm_trie_key` + 4-byte IPv4 prefix data. |
| `throttle_state` | LRU Hash | Per-IP packet counters + timestamps |

### Userspace (`xdp/controller.go`)

- Attaches XDP program to interface on startup (native mode → generic fallback if driver unsupported)
- `SyncLoop()` polls Valkey for blocklist updates every 30s and writes to `blocked_ips` BPF map
- No Valkey dependency at packet drop time — BPF map is the runtime source of truth
- Exports `ebpf.xdp.dropped_packets` OTel counter
- Detaches cleanly on `SIGTERM` / `Stop()`

**Relationship to existing rate limiting:** Complements (does not replace) the Valkey sliding-window rate limiter in `internal/middleware/`. XDP acts before TCP; middleware acts after HTTP parse.

**Fallback:** If XDP attach fails (missing `CAP_NET_ADMIN`, unsupported NIC driver), logs warning and falls back to app-layer rate limiting only. Nothing crashes.

---

## Configuration

All flags live in a new `EBPFConfig` struct added to `internal/config/config.go`, loaded via Viper.

```go
type EBPFConfig struct {
    ObservabilityEnabled  bool
    AuditEnabled          bool
    AuditIPAllowlist      []string
    AuditExecAllowlist    []string
    AuditRingBufferSize   int      // bytes, must be power of 2
    XDPEnabled            bool
    XDPInterface          string
}
```

| Env var | Default | Feature |
|---------|---------|---------|
| `RAVEN_EBPF_OBSERVABILITY_ENABLED` | `false` | #122 kprobe metrics |
| `RAVEN_EBPF_AUDIT_ENABLED` | `false` | #123 audit trail |
| `RAVEN_EBPF_AUDIT_IP_ALLOWLIST` | `""` | Comma-sep CIDRs |
| `RAVEN_EBPF_AUDIT_EXEC_ALLOWLIST` | `""` | Comma-sep binary paths |
| `RAVEN_EBPF_AUDIT_RING_BUFFER_SIZE` | `1048576` | Ring buffer bytes (power of 2) |
| `RAVEN_EBPF_XDP_ENABLED` | `false` | #120 XDP drop |
| `RAVEN_EBPF_XDP_INTERFACE` | `eth0` | NIC to attach XDP to |

All features default to `false` — opt-in only, safe for existing deployments.

---

## Build Pipeline Changes

### Dockerfile (builder stage)

Change the builder stage (currently `CGO_ENABLED=0`):
```dockerfile
RUN apk add --no-cache clang llvm linux-headers libbpf-dev musl-dev
RUN go install github.com/cilium/ebpf/cmd/bpf2go@latest
# CGO_ENABLED=1 required for cilium/ebpf userspace bindings
# -extldflags "-static" preserves fully static binary for Alpine runtime stage
RUN CGO_ENABLED=1 go build -ldflags="-s -w -extldflags '-static'" -o /api ./cmd/api
```

Runtime stage unchanged — BPF bytecode embedded at build time, binary remains fully static.

### CGO & Makefile.edge

`CGO_ENABLED=0` (current) breaks `cilium/ebpf`. `Makefile.edge` must split targets:

```makefile
# Existing non-eBPF build (unchanged)
build-arm64:
    CGO_ENABLED=0 GOOS=linux GOARCH=arm64 \
        go build -ldflags="$(LDFLAGS)" -o $(BUILD_DIR)/$(APP_NAME)-linux-arm64 ./cmd/api

# New eBPF-enabled build
build-arm64-ebpf:
    CC=aarch64-linux-musl-gcc CGO_ENABLED=1 GOOS=linux GOARCH=arm64 \
        go build -ldflags="$(LDFLAGS) -extldflags '-static'" \
        -o $(BUILD_DIR)/$(APP_NAME)-linux-arm64-ebpf ./cmd/api
```

Non-eBPF edge builds are unaffected.

### go generate & Generated Files

Each feature package contains a `//go:generate` directive:
```go
//go:generate go run github.com/cilium/ebpf/cmd/bpf2go -target amd64,arm64 Observability ../programs/observability.c
```

**Generated `_bpfel.go`/`_bpfeb.go` files are committed to the repository** (standard cilium/ebpf practice). Edge nodes require no C toolchain at runtime.

CI workflow (`.github/workflows/go.yml`) additions needed:
- Install `clang` on the runner: `sudo apt-get install -y clang llvm libbpf-dev`
- Add a `make generate && git diff --exit-code` step to verify generated files are up-to-date
- eBPF integration tests run on a separate `ebpf-integration` job with `--privileged` Docker

---

## Docker / Deployment Changes

Both `docker-compose.yml` and `docker-compose.edge.yml` — `go-api` service:

```yaml
cap_add:
  - CAP_BPF        # eBPF program loading, map creation (kernel >= 5.8)
  - CAP_NET_ADMIN  # XDP attachment only; omit if XDP disabled
```

`CAP_SYS_ADMIN` is **not** added — it is excessively broad. Minimum supported kernel is 5.8 where `CAP_BPF` suffices. `capabilities.go` detects the kernel version at startup and logs a clear error if the floor is not met rather than relying on container capabilities to paper over it.

**Kernel floor:** >= 5.8 with `CONFIG_DEBUG_INFO_BTF=y`. Raspberry Pi OS Bullseye (5.15 LTS) and Bookworm (6.1 LTS) both qualify.

**Prerequisite check on edge nodes:**
```bash
uname -r                          # must be >= 5.8
ls /sys/kernel/btf/vmlinux        # must exist (BTF enabled)
```

---

## Testing Strategy

| Layer | Approach |
|-------|----------|
| Unit | Mock `cilium/ebpf` interfaces; test config parsing, map helpers, graceful-degrade paths |
| Integration | Linux VM (GitHub Actions runner or local Docker with `--privileged`); load real BPF programs, verify maps populated |
| Observability | Assert OTel metrics appear in test meter provider after collector poll |
| XDP | Use `AF_XDP` loopback test or `veth` pair to send synthetic packets; verify drop counters |
| Audit | Exec a known binary in test; verify ring buffer emits correct event |

All eBPF integration tests are gated behind `//go:build linux,ebpf` build tag — skipped automatically on macOS and in environments without capabilities.

---

## Non-Goals

- No Windows or macOS eBPF support (Linux-only feature, gracefully no-ops elsewhere)
- No replacement of the Python AI worker observability (separate concern)
- No user-facing UI for audit logs in this milestone (raw OTLP pipeline output only)
- XDP does not replace the Valkey rate limiter — they are complementary layers

	var cfg Config
	if err := v.Unmarshal(&cfg); err != nil {
		return nil, err
	}

	if cfg.RateLimit.DefaultUserLimit <= 0 {
		return nil, fmt.Errorf("ratelimit.default_user_limit must be > 0, got %d", cfg.RateLimit.DefaultUserLimit)
	}
	if cfg.RateLimit.DefaultOrgLimit <= 0 {
		return nil, fmt.Errorf("ratelimit.default_org_limit must be > 0, got %d", cfg.RateLimit.DefaultOrgLimit)
	}

	return &cfg, nil

	chatAPI := router.Group("/api/v1/chat")
	chatAPI.Use(middleware.APIKeyAuth(&apiKeyLookupAdapter{repo: apiKeyRepo}))
	chatAPI.Use(middleware.SecurityRulesMiddleware(&securityEvaluatorAdapter{svc: securitySvc}))
	chatAPI.Use(middleware.StrangerCheck(strangerSvc, valkeyClient))
	{
		chatAPI.POST("/:kb_id/completions", chatHandler.StreamCompletion)
		chatAPI.GET("/:kb_id/sessions", chatHandler.ListSessions)
		chatAPI.GET("/:kb_id/sessions/:session_id/history", chatHandler.GetHistory)
		chatAPI.DELETE("/:kb_id/sessions/:session_id", chatHandler.DeleteSession)
	}

	// Internal routes — no JWT, no rate limiting.
	// Must only be reachable from the compose-internal network (enforce via firewall/network policy).
	internal := router.Group("/api/v1/internal")
	{
		internal.POST("/keycloak-webhook", userHandler.KeycloakWebhook)
	}

	// Create HTTP server
	addr := fmt.Sprintf(":%d", cfg.Server.Port)
	srv := &http.Server{
		Addr:    addr,
		Handler: router,
	}

	// Graceful shutdown with signal handling
	ctx, stop := signal.NotifyContext(context.Background(), syscall.SIGINT, syscall.SIGTERM)
	defer stop()

	// Start server in a goroutine
	go func() {
		log.Printf("Raven API server starting on %s", addr)
		if err := srv.ListenAndServe(); err != nil && err != http.ErrServerClosed {
			log.Fatalf("failed to start server: %v", err)
		}
	}()

	// Wait for interrupt signal
	<-ctx.Done()
	log.Println("shutting down server...")

	// Give outstanding requests 5 seconds to complete
	shutdownCtx, cancel := context.WithTimeout(context.Background(), 5*time.Second)
	defer cancel()

	if err := srv.Shutdown(shutdownCtx); err != nil {
		log.Fatalf("server forced to shutdown: %v", err)
	}

	log.Println("server exited gracefully")
}

	cfg, err := config.Load()
	if err != nil {

	}

	// Initialise OpenTelemetry (no-op when disabled or no endpoint).

	otelShutdown, err := telemetry.InitProvider(

		cfg.Server.Mode,
	)
	if err != nil {

	}
	defer func() {
		if err := otelShutdown(context.Background()); err != nil {
			log.Printf("telemetry shutdown error: %v", err)
		}
	}()

	// Set Gin mode
	gin.SetMode(cfg.Server.Mode)

	// Initialise Valkey client for rate limiting.
	valkeyClient := redis.NewClient(&redis.Options{
		Addr: cfg.Valkey.URL,
	})

## telemetry OTel MeterProvider

### Telemetry package
# Telemetry package

// Package telemetry initialises OpenTelemetry tracing and metrics for the
// Raven API.  When no OTLP endpoint is configured the package gracefully
// degrades to no-op providers so the rest of the application works without
// any observable overhead.
package telemetry

import (
	"context"
	"errors"

	"go.opentelemetry.io/otel"
	"go.opentelemetry.io/otel/exporters/otlp/otlpmetric/otlpmetricgrpc"
	"go.opentelemetry.io/otel/exporters/otlp/otlptrace/otlptracegrpc"
	"go.opentelemetry.io/otel/propagation"
	"go.opentelemetry.io/otel/sdk/metric"
	"go.opentelemetry.io/otel/sdk/resource"
	"go.opentelemetry.io/otel/sdk/trace"
	semconv "go.opentelemetry.io/otel/semconv/v1.26.0"
)

// InitProvider sets up a TracerProvider and MeterProvider.
//
// If endpoint is non-empty, OTLP gRPC exporters are configured for both traces
// and metrics.  Otherwise no-op providers are used so that callers never need
// to nil-check.
//
// The returned shutdown function flushes pending telemetry and releases
// resources.  It is safe to call even when the providers are no-ops.
func InitProvider(ctx context.Context, serviceName, serviceVersion, endpoint, environment string) (shutdown func(context.Context) error, err error) {
	// Build the OTel resource that describes this service.
	// We use NewSchemaless to avoid schema-URL conflicts with resource.Default().
	res, err := resource.Merge(
		resource.Default(),
		resource.NewSchemaless(
			semconv.ServiceName(serviceName),
			semconv.ServiceVersion(serviceVersion),
			semconv.Deploymen…

… := otlptracegrpc.New(ctx,
		otlptracegrpc.WithEndpoint(endpoint),
		otlptracegrpc.WithInsecure(),
	)
	if err != nil {
		return shutdownAll, err
	}

	tp := trace.NewTracerProvider(
		trace.WithBatcher(traceExporter),
		trace.WithResource(res),
	)
	shutdownFuncs = append(shutdownFuncs, tp.Shutdown)
	otel.SetTracerProvider(tp)

	// --- Metric exporter ---
	metricExporter, err := otlpmetricgrpc.New(ctx,
		otlpmetricgrpc.WithEndpoint(endpoint),
		otlpmetricgrpc.WithInsecure(),
	)
	if err != nil {
		return shutdownAll, err
	}

	mp := metric.NewMeterProvider(
		metric.WithReader(metric.NewPeriodicReader(metricExporter)),
		metric.WithResource(res),
	)
	shutdownFuncs = append(shutdownFuncs, mp.Shutdown)
	otel.SetMeterProvider(mp)

	// --- Propagation ---
	otel.SetTextMapPropagator(propagation.NewCompositeTextMapPropagator(
		propagation.TraceContext{},
		propagation.Baggage{},
	))

	return shutdownAll, nil
}

// securityEvaluatorAdapter bridges the SecurityService to the middleware.SecurityEvaluator
// interface without creating a circular import dependency.
type securityEvaluatorAdapter struct {
	svc *service.SecurityService
}

func (a *securityEvaluatorAdapter) EvaluateRequest(ctx context.Context, orgID, clientIP, path, method, userAgent string) (*middleware.SecurityRuleAction, error) {
	action, err := a.svc.EvaluateRequest(ctx, orgID, clientIP, path, method, userAgent)
	if err != nil {
		return nil, err
	}
	if action == nil {
		return nil, nil
	}
	return &middleware.SecurityRuleAction{
		Block:    action.Block,
		Throttle: action.Throttle,
		RuleID:   action.RuleID,
		RuleName: action.RuleName,
	}, nil
}

// apiKeyLookupAdapter bridges the APIKeyRepository to the middleware.APIKeyLookup
// interface without creating a circular import dependency.
type apiKeyLookupAdapter struct {
	repo *repository.APIKeyRepository
}

func (a *apiKeyLookupAdapter) LookupByHash(ctx context.Context, keyHash string) (*middleware.APIKeyLookupResult, error) {
	ak, err := a.repo.GetByKeyHashNoTx(ctx, keyHash)
	if err != nil {
		return nil, err
	}
	return apiKeyToLookupResult(ak), nil
}

func apiKeyToLookupResult(ak *model.APIKey) *middleware.APIKeyLookupResult {
	return &middleware.APIKeyLookupResult{
		ID:              ak.ID,
		OrgID:           ak.OrgID,
		WorkspaceID:     ak.WorkspaceID,
		KnowledgeBaseID: ak.KnowledgeBaseID,
		AllowedDomains:  ak.AllowedDomains,
		RateLimit:       ak.RateLimit,
		Status:          string(ak.Status),
	}
}

### docker compose -f docker-compose.edge.yml --env-file .env.edge up -d
#   docker compose -f docker-compose.edge.yml --env-file .env.edge up -d

services:
  # ─── Go API ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  go-api:
    image: ${RAVEN_API_IMAGE:-ghcr.io/ravencloak-org/raven-api:latest}
    build:
      context: .
      dockerfile: Dockerfile
      platforms:
        - linux/arm64
    ports:
      - "8080:8080"
    environment:
      RAVEN_SERVER_PORT: ${RAVEN_SERVER_PORT:-8080}
      RAVEN_DATABASE_URL: ${DATABASE_URL:?DATABASE_URL is required}
      RAVEN_GRPC_WORKER_ADDR: ${GRPC_AI_WORKER_ADDR:?GRPC_AI_WORKER_ADDR is required}
      RAVEN_EDGE_MODE: "true"
    depends_on:
      postgres:
        condition: service_healthy
    healthcheck:
      test: ["CMD", "wget", "--no-verbose", "--tries=1", "--spider", "http://localhost:8080/healthz"]
      interval: 10s
      timeout: 5s
      retries: 5
      start_period: 15s
    networks:
      - raven-edge
    labels:
      - "traefik.enable=true"
      - "traefik.http.routers.api.rule=PathPrefix(`/api`)"
      - "traefik.http.routers.api.entrypoints=web,websecure"
      - "traefik.http.services.api.loadbalancer.server.port=8080"
    restart: unless-stopped
    deploy:
      resources:
        limits:
          memory: 256M

  # ─── PostgreSQL + pgvector ─────────────────────────────────────────────────
  postgres:
    image: pgvector/pgvector:pg18
    platform: linux/arm64
    environment:
      POSTGRES_DB: ${POSTGRES_DB:-raven}
      POSTGRES_USER: ${POSTGRES_USER:-raven}
      POSTGRES_PASSWORD: ${POSTGRES_PASSWORD:?POSTGRES_PASSWORD is required}
    volumes:
      - pg-data:/var/lib/postgresql/data
      - ./deploy/postgres/init.sql:/docker-entrypoint-initdb.d/init.sql:ro
    healthcheck:
      test: ["CMD-SHELL", "pg_isready -U ${POSTGRES_USER:-raven} -d ${POSTGRES_DB:-raven}"]
      interval: 5s
      timeout: 5s
      retries: 5
    networks:
      - raven-edge
    restart: unless-stopped
    deploy:
      resources:
        limits:
          memory: 512M

  # ─── Traefik Reverse Proxy ────────────────────────────────────────────────
  traefik:
    image: traefik:v3.3
    platform: linux/arm64
    command:
      - "--api.insecure=true"
      - "--providers.docker=true"
      - "--providers.docker.exposedbydefault=false"
      - "--providers.docker.network=raven-edge"
      - "--entrypoints.web.address=:80"
      - "--entrypoints.websecure.address=:443"
      - "--log.level=WARN"
    ports:
      - "80:80"
      - "443:443"
      - "8082:8080"
    volumes:
      - /var/run/docker.sock:/var/run/docker.sock:ro
    networks:
      - raven-edge
    restart: unless-stopped
    deploy:
      resources:
        limits:
          memory: 128M

### Generate eBPF Go files using go generate

Source: https://pkg.go.dev/github.com/cilium/ebpf/cmd/bpf2go%40v0.11.0

Demonstrates how to invoke bpf2go via the go generate directive to compile a C source file into Go-compatible eBPF bytecode files. This command generates both little and big endian versions of the compiled BPF.

```go
//go:generate go run github.com/cilium/ebpf/cmd/bpf2go foo path/to/src.c -- -I/path/to/include
```

--------------------------------

### Kprobe Example using bpf2go

Source: https://pkg.go.dev/github.com/cilium/ebpf/examples

This example demonstrates attaching an eBPF program to the entry or exit of an arbitrary kernel symbol (function) using kprobes and the bpf2go tool. bpf2go simplifies the process of compiling eBPF programs written in C and loading them into the kernel from Go.

```go
// Example for kprobe using bpf2go
// ... (actual code would be here)

```

--------------------------------

### Tracepoint Example using bpf2go

Source: https://pkg.go.dev/github.com/cilium/ebpf/examples

This example shows how to attach an eBPF program to kernel tracepoints using bpf2go. Tracepoints are predefined points in the kernel code that can be used for debugging and performance analysis.

```go
// Example for tracepoint using bpf2go
// ... (actual code would be here)

```

--------------------------------

### Initialize BPF Ring Buffer Reader

Source: https://pkg.go.dev/github.com/cilium/ebpf/ringbuf

Creates a new Reader instance to interact with a BPF ring buffer map. It requires an initialized *ebpf.Map as input and returns a Reader pointer or an error.

```go
func NewReader(ringbufMap *ebpf.Map) (*Reader, error)
```

--------------------------------

### Attach eBPF programs to Kprobes and Tracepoints

Source: https://pkg.go.dev/github.com/cilium/ebpf/link?tab=versions

Functions to attach eBPF programs to kernel probes (Kprobes) or tracepoints. These functions return a Link interface that allows for closing or pinning the attachment.

```go
func Kprobe(symbol string, prog *ebpf.Program) (Link, error)
func Kretprobe(symbol string, prog *ebpf.Program) (Link, error)
func Tracepoint(group, name string, prog *ebpf.Program) (Link, error)
```

### Attach XDP Programs to Network Interfaces

Source: https://context7.com/cilium/ebpf/llms.txt

Shows how to attach an XDP program to a specific network interface for high-performance packet processing. It covers interface lookup, program attachment with specific flags, and monitoring packet counts via eBPF maps.

```go
package main

import (
    "log"
    "net"
    "os"
    "os/signal"
    "time"

    "github.com/cilium/ebpf/link"
    "github.com/cilium/ebpf/rlimit"
)

//go:generate go tool bpf2go -target amd64 xdp xdp.c

func main() {
    if err := rlimit.RemoveMemlock(); err != nil {
        log.Fatal(err)
    }

    var objs xdpObjects
    if err := loadXdpObjects(&objs, nil); err != nil {
        log.Fatalf("loading objects: %v", err)
    }
    defer objs.Close()

    // Get interface by name
    iface, err := net.InterfaceByName("eth0")
    if err != nil {
        log.Fatalf("getting interface: %v", err)
    }

    // Attach XDP program to interface
    xdpLink, err := link.AttachXDP(link.XDPOptions{
        Program:   objs.XdpProg,
        Interface: iface.Index,
        Flags:     link.XDPGenericMode, // Use XDPDriverMode for native mode
    })
    if err != nil {
        log.Fatalf("attaching XDP: %v", err)
    }
    defer xdpLink.Close()

    log.Printf("XDP program attached to %s", iface.Name)

    // Read packet counts
    stop := make(chan os.Signal, 1)
    signal.Notify(stop, os.Interrupt)

    ticker := time.NewTicker(time.Second)
    defer ticker.Stop()

    for {
        select {
        case <-ticker.C:
            var count uint64
            if err := objs.PktCount.Lookup(uint32(0), &count); err != nil {
                log.Printf("map lookup: %v", err)
                continue
            }
            log.Printf("Packets: %d", count)
        case <-stop:
            return
        }
    }
}
```

--------------------------------

### Read events from eBPF using Ring Buffers

Source: https://context7.com/cilium/ebpf/llms.txt

Demonstrates how to initialize a ring buffer reader to consume events from an eBPF map. This approach is preferred for lower overhead compared to perf event arrays.

```Go
package main

import (
	"bytes"
	"encoding/binary"
	"errors"
	"log"
	"os"
	"os/signal"

	"github.com/cilium/ebpf/link"
	"github.com/cilium/ebpf/ringbuf"
	"github.com/cilium/ebpf/rlimit"
)

//go:generate go tool bpf2go -target amd64 ringbuf ringbuffer.c

type Event struct {
	Pid  uint32
	Comm [16]byte
}

func main() {
	if err := rlimit.RemoveMemlock(); err != nil {
		log.Fatal(err)
	}

	var objs ringbufObjects
	if err := loadRingbufObjects(&objs, nil); err != nil {
		log.Fatalf("loading objects: %v", err)
	}
	defer objs.Close()

	kp, err := link.Kprobe("sys_execve", objs.KprobeExecve, nil)
	if err != nil {
		log.Fatalf("attaching kprobe: %v", err)
	}
	defer kp.Close()

	rd, err := ringbuf.NewReader(objs.Events)
	if err != nil {
		log.Fatalf("creating ringbuf reader: %v", err)
	}
	defer rd.Close()

	stop := make(chan os.Signal, 1)
	signal.Notify(stop, os.Interrupt)

	go func() {
		<-stop
		rd.Close()
	}()

	log.Println("Waiting for events...")

	var event Event
	for {
		record, err := rd.Read()
		if err != nil {
			if errors.Is(err, ringbuf.ErrClosed) {
				log.Println("Ring buffer closed, exiting")
				return
			}
			log.Printf("reading from ringbuf: %v", err)
			continue
		}

		if err := binary.Read(bytes.NewBuffer(record.RawSample), binary.LittleEndian, &event); err != nil {
			log.Printf("parsing event: %v", err)
			continue
		}

		comm := string(bytes.TrimRight(event.Comm[:], "\x00"))
		log.Printf("PID: %d, Command: %s", event.Pid, comm)
	}
}
```

--------------------------------

### Attach Kprobes and Kretprobes using Go

Source: https://context7.com/cilium/ebpf/llms.txt

Demonstrates attaching eBPF programs to kernel function entry and return points using the cilium/ebpf link package. It includes memory limit configuration, object loading, and periodic map lookups to monitor function calls.

```go
package main

import (
    "log"
    "time"

    "github.com/cilium/ebpf/link"
    "github.com/cilium/ebpf/rlimit"
)

//go:generate go tool bpf2go -target amd64 kprobe kprobe.c

func main() {
    if err := rlimit.RemoveMemlock(); err != nil {
        log.Fatal(err)
    }

    var objs kprobeObjects
    if err := loadKprobeObjects(&objs, nil); err != nil {
        log.Fatalf("loading objects: %v", err)
    }
    defer objs.Close()

    // Attach kprobe to sys_execve (program entry)
    kp, err := link.Kprobe("sys_execve", objs.KprobeExecve, nil)
    if err != nil {
        log.Fatalf("attaching kprobe: %v", err)
    }
    defer kp.Close()

    // Attach kretprobe (function return)
    kret, err := link.Kretprobe("sys_execve", objs.KretprobeExecve, &link.KprobeOptions{
        Cookie: 0x1234, // Arbitrary value accessible via bpf_get_attach_cookie()
    })
    if err != nil {
        log.Fatalf("attaching kretprobe: %v", err)
    }
    defer kret.Close()

    // Read from map periodically
    ticker := time.NewTicker(time.Second)
    defer ticker.Stop()

    for range ticker.C {
        var count uint64
        if err := objs.CountMap.Lookup(uint32(0), &count); err != nil {
            log.Fatalf("reading map: %v", err)
        }
        log.Printf("sys_execve called %d times", count)
    }
}
```

### eBPF Go

Source: https://context7.com/cilium/ebpf/llms.txt

eBPF Go (ebpf-go) is a pure Go library that provides a comprehensive toolkit for interacting with eBPF programs from Go applications. It enables developers to load eBPF bytecode into the kernel, manage data exchange via eBPF maps, and attach programs to various kernel hooks such as kprobes, tracepoints, and XDP. Designed with minimal external dependencies, the library is well-suited for long-running processes and includes specialized packages for assembling instructions, reading BTF information, and handling perf event arrays or ring buffers.

--------------------------------

### Resource Limits

Source: https://github.com/cilium/ebpf/blob/main/docs/ebpf/concepts/rlimit.md

Creating eBPF objects like Maps, Programs, and BTF blobs requires kernel memory allocation. Historically, before kernel version 5.11, the memory available for these allocations was governed by the `RLIMIT_MEMLOCK` rlimit value, which could be checked using `ulimit -l`. However, starting with kernel version 5.11, the Linux kernel transitioned to using memory cgroups (memcg) for managing memory limits related to eBPF objects. In this new model, eBPF allocations are accounted for within the cgroup's memory limits, similar to other process memory usage. These limits can be monitored and adjusted via cgroupfs, the standard interface for container memory controls.
```

linux\n\n// Package ebpf owns the eBPF lifecycle for the Raven API.\n// All features degrade gracefully to no-op when eBPF is unavailable.\npackage ebpf\n\nimport (\n\t\"fmt\"\n\t\"log/slog\"\n\t\"os\"\n\t\"runtime\"\n\t\"strconv\"\n\t\"strings\"\n\n\t\"github.com/cilium/ebpf/rlimit\"\n)\n\n// ErrUnsupported is returned when the runtime environment cannot support eBPF.\ntype ErrUnsupported struct {\n\tReason string\n}\n\nfunc (e *ErrUnsupported) Error() string {\n\treturn fmt.Sprintf(\"eBPF unavailable: %s\", e.Reason)\n}\n\n// minKernelVersion is the minimum supported kernel (5.8).\nconst minKernelMajor, minKernelMinor = 5, 8\n\n// CheckCapabilities verifies that the runtime environment supports eBPF.\n// It checks the kernel version floor, calls rlimit.RemoveMemlock() on kernels\n// < 5.11, and returns ErrUnsupported when the environment cannot run eBPF.\n// On non-Linux platforms this always returns ErrUnsupported.\nfunc CheckCapabilities() error {\n\tif runtime.GOOS != \"linux\" {\n\t\treturn &ErrUnsupported{Reason: \"non-Linux OS: \" + runtime.GOOS}\n\t}\n\n\tmajor, minor, err := kernelVersion()\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn &ErrUnsupported{Reason: \"cannot determine kernel version: \" + err.Error()}\n\t}\n\tif major < minKernelMajor || (major == minKernelMajor && minor < minKernelMinor) {\n\t\treturn &ErrUnsupported{\n\t\t\tReason: fmt.Sprintf(\"kernel %d.%d < required %d.%d\", major, minor, minKernelMajor, minKernelMinor),\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\t// Kernels < 5.11 require RLIMIT_MEMLOCK to be raised for BPF map allocation.\n\tif major < 5 || (major == 5 && minor < 11) {\n\t\tif err := rlimit.RemoveMemlock(); err != nil {\n\t\t\tslog.Warn(\"eBPF: failed to remove RLIMIT_MEMLOCK; map allocation may fail\", \"error\", err)\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\t// Check BTF availability — required for CO-RE and tp_btf programs.\n\tif _, err := os.Stat(\"/sys/kernel/btf/vmlinux\"); os.IsNotExist(err) {\n\t\treturn &ErrUnsupported{Reason: \"BTF not available (/sys/kernel/btf/vmlinux missing); ensure CONFIG_DEBUG_INFO_BTF=y\"}\n\t}\n\n\treturn nil\n}\n\n// kernelVersion parses /proc/version_signature or /proc/version and returns\n// the major and minor kernel version numbers.\nfunc kernelVersion() (major, minor int, err error) {\n\tdata, err := os.ReadFile(\"/proc/version\")\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn 0, 0, err\n\t}\n\t// Format: \"Linux version X.Y.Z ...\"\n\tfields := strings.Fields(string(data))\n\tif len(fields) < 3 {\n\t\treturn 0, 0, fmt.Errorf(\"unexpected /proc/version format\")\n\t}\n\tparts := strings.SplitN(fields[2], \".\", 3)\n\tif len(parts) < 2 {\n\t\treturn 0, 0, fmt.Errorf(\"cannot parse kernel version %q\", fields[2])\n\t}\n\tmajor, err = strconv.Atoi(parts[0])\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn 0, 0, fmt.Errorf(\"parsing major: %w\", err)\n\t}\n\tminor, err = strconv.Atoi(parts[1])\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn 0, 0, fmt.Errorf(\"parsing minor: %w\", err)\n\t}\n\treturn major, minor, nil\n}\n```\n\n- [ ] **Step 3A.3: Create stub.go for non-Linux**\n\n```go\n//go:build !linux\n\n// Package ebpf provides no-op stubs on non-Linux SPDX-License-Identifier: GPL-2.0\n\n#include \"vmlinux.h\"\n#include <bpf/bpf_helpers.h>\n#include <bpf/bpf_tracing.h>\n#include <bpf/bpf_core_read.h>\n\nchar LICENSE[] SEC(\"license\") = \"GPL\";\n\n// Per-PID CPU time accumulator (nanoseconds)\nstruct {\n    __uint(type, BPF_MAP_TYPE_HASH);\n    __uint(max_entries, 4096);\n    __type(key, u32);   // pid\n    __type(value, u64); // accumulated cpu ns\n} cpu_time_map SEC(\".maps\");\n\n// Network bytes in/out per PID\nstruct {\n    __uint(type, BPF_MAP_TYPE_HASH);\n    __uint(max_entries, 4096);\n    __type(key, u32);\n    __type(value, u64);\n} net_bytes_in SEC(\".maps\");\n\nstruct {\n    __uint(type, BPF_MAP_TYPE_HASH);\n    __uint(max_entries, 4096);\n    __type(key, u32);\n    __type(value, u64);\n} net_bytes_out SEC(\".maps\");\n\n// Syscall error counts (by syscall nr)\nstruct {\n    __uint(type, BPF_MAP_TYPE_HASH);\n    __uint(max_entries, 512);\n    __type(key, u32);   // syscall nr\n    __type(value, u64); // error count\n} syscall_errors SEC(\".maps\");\n\n// FD count per PID\nstruct {\n    __uint(type, BPF_MAP_TYPE_HASH);\n    __uint(max_entries, 4096);\n    __type(key, u32);\n    __type(value, u64);\n} fd_count_map SEC(\".maps\");\n\n// sched_switch: accumulate prev task CPU time delta\nSEC(\"tp_btf/sched_switch\")\nint BPF_PROG(handle_sched_switch, bool preempt,\n             struct task_struct *prev, struct task_struct *next)\n{\n    u32 pid = BPF_CORE_READ(prev, pid);\n    u64 runtime = BPF_CORE_READ(prev, se.sum_exec_runtime);\n    u64 *val = bpf_map_lookup_elem(&cpu_time_map, &pid);\n    if (val)\n        __sync_fetch_and_add(val, runtime);\n    else\n        bpf_map_update_elem(&cpu_time_map, &pid, &runtime, BPF_ANY);\n    return 0;\n}\n\n// raw_syscalls/sys_exit: count non-zero return codes as errors\nSEC(\"tracepoint/raw_syscalls/sys_exit\")\nint handle_sys_exit(struct trace_event_raw_sys_exit *ctx)\n{\n    if (ctx->ret >= 0) return 0;\n    u32 nr = (u32)ctx->id;\n    u64 one = 1;\n    u64 *val = bpf_map_lookup_elem(&syscall_errors, &nr);\n    if (val)\n        __sync_fetch_and_add(val, 1);\n    else\n        bpf_map_update_elem(&syscall_errors, &nr, &one, BPF_ANY);\n    return 0;\n}\n\n// net/netif_receive_skb: bytes in per PID (approximate — skb owner)\nSEC(\"tracepoint/net/netif_receive_skb\")\nint handle_net_rx(struct trace_event_raw_netif_receive_skb *ctx)\n{\n    u32 pid = bpf_get_current_pid_tgid() >> 32;\n    u64 len = ctx->len;\n    u64 *val = bpf_map_lookup_elem(&net_bytes_in, &pid);\n    if (val)\n        __sync_fetch_and_add(val, len);\n    else\n        bpf_map_update_elem(&net_bytes_in, &pid, &len, BPF_ANY);\n    return 0;\n}\n\n// net/net_dev_start_xmit: bytes out per PID\nSEC(\"tracepoint/net/net_dev_start_xmit\")\nint handle_net_tx(struct trace_event_raw_net_dev_start_xmit *ctx)\n{\n    u32 pid = bpf_get_current_pid_tgid() >> 32;\n    u64 len = ctx->len;\n    u64 *val = bpf_map_lookup_elem(&net_bytes_out, &pid);\n    if (val)\n        __sync_fetch_and_add(val, len);\n    else\n        bpf_map_update_elem(&net_bytes_out, &pid, &len, BPF_ANY);\n    return 0;\n}\n\n// kprobe/__fd_install: track FD creation per PID\nSEC(\"kprobe/__fd_install\")\nint BPF_KPROBE(handle_fd_install)\n{\n    u32 pid = bpf_get_current_pid_tgid() >> 32;\n    u64 one = 1;\n    u64 *val = bpf_map_lookup_elem(&fd_count_map, &pid);\n    if (val)\n        __sync_fetch_and_add(val, 1);\n    else\n        bpf_map_update_elem(&fd_count_map, &pid, &one, BPF_ANY);\n    return 0;\n}\n```\n\n- [ ] **Step 4.2: Create programs/audit.c**\n\n```c\n// SPDX-License-Identifier: GPL-2.0\n\n#include \"vmlinux.h\"\n#include <bpf/bpf_helpers.h>\n#include <bpf/bpf_tracing.h>\n#include <bpf/bpf_core_read.h>\n\nchar LICENSE[] SEC(\"license\") = \"GPL\";\n\n#define TASK_COMM_LEN 16\n#define PATH_LEN      128\n\n// Audit event types\n#define AUDIT_EXEC    1\n#define AUDIT_TCP     2\n#define AUDIT_CONNECT 3\n\nstruct audit_event {\n    u8  type;\n    u32 pid;\n    u32 ppid;\n    u64 timestamp_ns;\n    char comm[TASK_COMM_LEN];\n    union {\n        struct {\n            char path[PATH_LEN];\n        } exec;\n        struct {\n            __be32 saddr;\n            __be32 daddr;\n            __be16 sport;\n            __be16 dport;\n        } net;\n    };\n};\n\n// Ring buffer for audit events — sized at load time by userspace\nstruct {\n    __uint(type, BPF_MAP_TYPE_RINGBUF);\n    __uint(max_entries, 1 << 20); // 1MB default; overridden by userspace\n} audit_events SEC(\".maps\");\n\nSEC(\"tracepoint/syscalls/sys_enter_execve\")\nint handle_execve(struct trace_event_raw_sys_enter *ctx)\n{\n    struct audit_event *e = bpf_ringbuf_reserve(&audit_events, sizeof(*e), 0);\n    if (!e) return 0;\n\n    e->type = AUDIT_EXEC;\n    e->pid  = bpf_get_current_pid_tgid() >> 32;\n    e->timestamp_ns = bpf_ktime_get_ns();\n    bpf_get_current_comm(e->comm, sizeof(e->comm));\n\n    struct task_struct *task = (struct task_struct *)bpf_get_current_task();\n    e->ppid = BPF_CORE_READ(task, real_parent, pid);\n\n    // Read first argument (filename pointer)\n    const char *filename = (const char *)ctx->args[0];\n    bpf_probe_read_user_str(e->exec.path, sizeof(e->exec.path), filename);\n\n    bpf_ringbuf_submit(e, 0);\n    return 0;\n}\n\nSEC(\"tracepoint/sock/inet_sock_set_state\")\nint handle_tcp_state(struct trace_event_raw_inet_sock_set_state *ctx)\n{\n    // Only care about transitions TO TCP_ESTABLISHED\n    if (ctx->newstate != 1 /* TCP_ESTABLISHED */) return 0;\n\n    struct audit_event *e = bpf_ringbuf_reserve(&audit_events, sizeof(*e), 0);\n    if (!e) return 0;\n\n    e->type = AUDIT_TCP;\n    e->pid  = bpf_get_current_pid_tgid() >> 32;\n    e->timestamp_ns = bpf_ktime_get_ns();\n    bpf_get_current_comm(e->comm, sizeof(e->comm));\n    e->net.saddr = ctx->saddr;\n    e->net.daddr = ctx->daddr;\n    e->net.sport = ctx->sport;\n    e->net.dport = ctx->dport;\n\n    bpf_ringbuf_submit(e, 0);\n    return 0;\n}\n\nSEC(\"tracepoint/syscalls/sys_enter_connect\")\nint handle_connect(struct trace_event_raw_sys_enter *ctx)\n{\n    struct audit_event *e = bpf_ringbuf_reserve(&audit_events, sizeof(*e), 0);\n    if (!e) return 0;\n\n    e->type = AUDIT_CONNECT;\n    e->pid  = bpf_get_current_pid_tgid() >> 32;\n    e->timestamp_ns = bpf_ktime_get_ns();\n    bpf_get_current_comm(e->comm, sizeof(e->comm));\n\n    bpf_ringbuf_submit(e, 0);\n    return 0;\n}\n```\n\n- [ ] **Step 4.3: Create programs/xdp.c**\n\n```c\n// SPDX-License-Identifier: GPL-2.0\n\n#include \"vmlinux.h\"\n#include <bpf/bpf_helpers.h>\n\nchar LICENSE[] SEC(\"license\") = \"GPL\";\n\n// LPM trie key for IPv4 prefix matching\nstruct lpm_key {\n    __u32 prefixlen;\n    __u32 addr;\n};\n\n// Blocked CIDRs — LPM trie for prefix matching\nstruct {\n    __uint(type, BPF_MAP_TYPE_LPM_TRIE);\n    __uint(max_entries, 10000);\n    __uint(map_flags, BPF_F_NO_PREALLOC);\n    __type(key, struct lpm_key);\n    __type(value, u32); // reason code (reserved)\n} blocked_ips SEC(\".maps\");\n\n// Throttle state — per-IP packet counter + timestamp (LRU evicts stale entries)\nstruct throttle_val {\n    u64 count;\n    u64 window_start_ns;\n};\n\nstruct {\n    __uint(type, BPF_MAP_TYPE_LRU_HASH);\n    __uint(max_entries, 65536);\n    __type(key, u32);   // src IP\n    __type(value, struct throttle_val);\n} throttle_state SEC(\".maps\");\n\n// XDP drop counter\nstruct {\n    __uint(type, BPF_MAP_TYPE_ARRAY);\n    __uint(max_entries, 1);\n    __type(key, u32);\n    __type(value, u64);\n} drop_count SEC(\".maps\");\n\n#define THROTTLE_LIMIT  1000   // packets per window\n#define THROTTLE_WINDOW 1000000000ULL // 1 second in ns\n\nSEC(\"xdp\")\nint xdp_filter(struct xdp_md *ctx)\n{\n    void *data_end = (void *)(long)ctx->data_end;\n    void *data     = (void *)(long)ctx->data;\n\n    // Parse Ethernet header\n    struct ethhdr *eth = data;\n    if ((void *)(eth + 1) > data_end) return XDP_PASS;\n    if (eth->h_proto != __constant_htons(ETH_P_IP)) return XDP_PASS;\n\n    // Parse IP header\n    struct iphdr *ip = (void *)(eth + 1);\n    if ((void *)(ip + 1) > data_end) return XDP_PASS;\n\n    u32 src = ip->saddr;\n\n    // LPM trie lookup — check if src IP is in a blocked CIDR\n    struct lpm_key key = { .prefixlen = 32, .addr = src };\n    if (bpf_map_lookup_elem(&blocked_ips, &key)) {\n        u32 idx = 0;\n        u64 *cnt = bpf_map_lookup_elem(&drop_count, &idx);\n        if (cnt) __sync_fetch_and_add(cnt, 1);\n        return XDP_DROP;\n    }\n\n    // Throttle check\n    u64 now = bpf_ktime_get_ns();\n    struct throttle_val *tv = bpf_map_lookup_elem(&throttle_state, &src);\n    if (tv) {\n        if (now - tv->window_start_ns > THROTTLE_WINDOW) {\n            tv->window_start_ns = now;\n            tv->count = 1;\n        } else {\n            __sync_fetch_and_add(&tv->count, 1);\n            if (tv->count > THROTTLE_LIMIT) {\n                u32 idx = 0;\n                u64 *cnt = bpf_map_lookup_elem(&drop_count, &idx);\n                if (cnt) __sync_fetch_and_add(cnt, 1);\n                return XDP_DROP;\n            }\n        }\n    } else {\n        struct throttle_val new_tv = { .count = 1, .window_start_ns = now };\n        bpf_map_update_elem(&throttle_state, &src, &new_tv, BPF_ANY);\n    }\n\n    return XDP_PASS;\n}\n```\n\n- [ ] **Step 4.4: When maps is nil (no BPF objects loaded), all metrics report zero and the\n// collector is a no-op — safe on kernels without eBPF support.\npackage observability\n\nimport (\n\t\"context\"\n\t\"io\"\n\t\"log/slog\"\n\t\"time\"\n\n\t\"go.opentelemetry.io/otel/metric\"\n)\n\n// Maps holds the BPF map handles needed by the collector.\n// It is populated from the bpf2go-generated objects after loading.\n// When nil, the collector runs as a no-op.\ntype Maps struct {\n\tCPUTimeMap    MapReader\n\tNetBytesIn    MapReader\n\tNetBytesOut   MapReader\n\tSyscallErrors MapReader\n\tFDCountMap    MapReader\n}\n\n// MapReader is the subset of ebpf.Map used by the collector (easy to mock).\ntype MapReader interface {\n\t// Iterate calls fn for each key-value pair in the map.\n\t// For testing, a nil MapReader is treated as an empty map.\n}\n\n// Collector polls BPF maps on a configurable interval and reports OTel metrics.\ntype Collector struct {\n\tmeter    metric.Meter\n\tmaps     *Maps\n\tinterval time.Duration\n\tstop     chan struct{}\n\tdone     chan struct{}\n\n\tcpuTime     metric.Int64Counter\n\tnetBytesIn  metric.Int64Counter\n\tnetBytesOut metric.Int64Counter\n\tsyscallErrs metric.Int64Counter\n\tfdCount     metric.Int64ObservableGauge\n}\n\n// NewCollector creates a Collector that reports metrics via the given Meter.\n// Pass nil maps to get a no-op collector (graceful degrade).\nfunc NewCollector(meter metric.Meter, maps *Maps) (*Collector, error) {\n\tc := &Collector{\n\t\tmeter:    meter,\n\t\tmaps:     maps,\n\t\tinterval: 15 * time.Second,\n\t\tstop:     make(chan struct{}),\n\t\tdone:     make(chan struct{}),\n\t}\n\n\tvar err error\n\tc.cpuTime, err = meter.Int64Counter(\n\t\t\"ebpf.process.cpu_time\",\n\t\tmetric.WithDescription(\"Accumulated CPU time per process (ms)\"),\n\t\tmetric.WithUnit(\"ms\"),\n\t)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, err\n\t}\n\tc.netBytesIn, err = meter.Int64Counter(\n\t\t\"ebpf.net.bytes_in\",\n\t\tmetric.WithDescription(\"Network bytes received per PID\"),\n\t\tmetric.WithUnit(\"By\"),\n\t)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, err\n\t}\n\tc.netBytesOut, err = meter.Int64Counter(\n\t\t\"ebpf.net.bytes_out\",\n\t\tmetric.WithDescription(\"Network bytes sent per PID\"),\n\t\tmetric.WithUnit(\"By\"),\n\t)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, err\n\t}\n\tc.syscallErrs, err = meter.Int64Counter(\n\t\t\"ebpf.syscall.errors\",\n\t\tmetric.WithDescription(\"Syscall error rate by syscall number\"),\n\t\tmetric.WithUnit(\"{count}\"),\n\t)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, err\n\t}\n\t_, err = meter.Int64ObservableGauge(\n\t\t\"ebpf.fd.count\",\n\t\tmetric.WithDescription(\"Open file descriptor count per PID\"),\n\t\tmetric.WithUnit(\"{fd}\"),\n\t)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, err\n\t}\n\n\treturn c, nil\n}\n\n// Start begins polling BPF maps in the background.\n// Safe to call even when maps is nil (no-op).\nfunc (c *Collector) Start(ctx context.Context) {\n\tgo c.run(ctx)\n}\n\nfunc (c *Collector) run(ctx context.Context) {\n\tdefer close(c.done)\n\tif c.maps == nil {\n\t\tslog.Debug(\"ebpf/observability: no BPF maps; collector is a no-op\")\n\t\treturn\n\t}\n\tticker := time.NewTicker(c.interval)\n\tdefer ticker.Stop()\n\tfor {\n\t\tselect {\n\t\tcase <-ticker.C:\n\t\t\tc.collect(ctx)\n\t\tcase <-c.stop:\n\t\t\treturn\n\t\tcase <-ctx.Done():\n\t\t\treturn\n\t\t}\n\t}\n}\n\nfunc (c *Collector) collect(_ context.Context) {\n\t// Real implementation iterates BPF maps and records metrics.\n\t// Wired fully in Task 8 after bpf2go generates the typed map accessors.\n\tslog.Debug(\"ebpf/observability: collect tick\")\n}\n\n// Close stops the collector. Implements io.Closer.\nfunc (c *Collector) Close() error {\n\tselect {\n\tcase <-c.stop:\n\tdefault:\n\t\tclose(c.stop)\n\t}\n\treturn nil\n}\n\nvar _ io.Closer = (*Collector)(nil)\n```\n\n- [ ] **Step 6.2: Create consumer.go**\n\n```go\n//go:build linux\n\n// Package audit implements Feature #123: security audit trail via eBPF ring buffer.\npackage audit\n\nimport (\n\t\"context\"\n\t\"encoding/binary\"\n\t\"errors\"\n\t\"fmt\"\n\t\"io\"\n\t\"log/slog\"\n\t\"net\"\n\t\"net/netip\"\n\n\t\"github.com/cilium/ebpf/ringbuf\"\n\t\"go.opentelemetry.io/otel/metric\"\n)\n\n// Config configures the audit consumer.\ntype Config struct {\n\tIPAllowlist   []string // allowed outbound CIDRs; empty = allow all\n\tExecAllowlist []string // allowed binary paths; empty = allow all\n}\n\n// RingBufReader is the subset of ringbuf.Reader used by Consumer (allows mocking).\ntype RingBufReader interface {\n\tRead() (ringbuf.Record, error)\n\tClose() error\n}\n\n// Consumer reads audit events from a BPF ring buffer and emits structured logs.\ntype Consumer struct {\n\treader      RingBufReader\n\tcfg         Config\n\tallowedNets []*net.IPNet\n\tstop        chan struct{}\n\tdone        chan struct{}\n\tdropped     metric.Int64Counter\n}\n\n// NewConsumer creates a Consumer. Pass nil reader for a no-op (graceful degrade).\nfunc NewConsumer(reader RingBufReader, meter metric.Meter, cfg Config) (*Consumer, error) {\n\tnets, err := parseCIDRs(cfg.IPAllowlist)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"audit: invalid IP allowlist: %w\", err)\n\t}\n\n\tdropped, err := meter.Int64Counter(\n\t\t\"ebpf.audit.dropped_events\",\n\t\tmetric.WithDescription(\"Audit ring buffer overflow drop count\"),\n\t\tmetric.WithUnit(\"{count}\"),\n\t)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, err\n\t}\n\n\treturn &Consumer{\n\t\treader:      reader,\n\t\tcfg:         cfg,\n\t\tallowedNets: nets,\n\t\tstop:        make(chan struct{}),\n\t\tdone:        make(chan struct{}),\n\t\tdropped:     dropped,\n\t}, nil\n}\n\n// Start begins consuming events in a background goroutine.\nfunc (c *Consumer) Start(ctx context.Context) {\n\tgo c.run(ctx)\n}\n\nfunc (c *Consumer) run(ctx context.Context) {\n\tdefer close(c.done)\n\tif c.reader == nil {\n\t\tslog.Debug(\"ebpf/audit: no ring buffer reader; consumer is a no-op\")\n\t\treturn\n\t}\n\tfor {\n\t\trecord, err := c.reader.Read()\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\tif errors.Is(err, ringbuf.ErrClosed) {\n\t\t\t\treturn\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\t// Ring buffer overflow — increment OTel counter and log.\n\t\t\tc.dropped.Add(ctx, 1)\n\t\t\tslog.Warn(\"ebpf/audit: ring buffer overflow; events dropped\", \"error\", err)\n\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t}\n\t\tc.handleRecord(ctx, record)\n\n\t\tselect {\n\t\tcase <-c.stop:\n\t\t\treturn\n\t\tcase <-ctx.Done():\n\t\t\treturn\n\t\tdefault:\n\t\t}\n\t}\n}\n\n// auditEventType mirrors the C enum in audit.c\nconst (\n\tauditExec    = 1\n\tauditTCP     = 2\n\tauditConnect = 3\n)\n\nfunc (c *Consumer) handleRecord(ctx context.Context, rec ringbuf.Record) {\n\tif len(rec.RawSample) < 1 {\n\t\treturn\n\t}\n\teventType := rec.RawSample[0]\n\n\tpid := binary.LittleEndian.Uint32(rec.RawSample[4:8])\n\tts := binary.LittleEndian.Uint64(rec.RawSample[12:20])\n\tcomm := nullTermStr(rec.RawSample[20:36])\n\n\tswitch eventType {\n\tcase auditExec:\n\t\tpath := nullTermStr(rec.RawSample[36:164])\n\t\tviolation := len(c.cfg.ExecAllowlist) > 0 && !sliceContains(c.cfg.ExecAllowlist, path)\n\t\tslog.InfoContext(ctx, \"ebpf/audit: exec\",\n\t\t\t\"pid\", pid, \"comm\", comm, \"path\", path,\n\t\t\t\"timestamp_ns\", ts, \"audit.violation\", violation,\n\t\t)\n\tcase auditTCP:\n\t\tif len(rec.RawSample) < 44 {\n\t\t\treturn\n\t\t}\n\t\tsaddr := netip.AddrFrom4([4]byte(rec.RawSample[36:40]))\n\t\tdaddr := netip.AddrFrom4([4]byte(rec.RawSample[40:44]))\n\t\tviolation := !c.ipAllowed(daddr.Unmap().String())\n\t\tslog.InfoContext(ctx, \"ebpf/audit: tcp-established\",\n\t\t\t\"pid\", pid, \"comm\", comm,\n\t\t\t\"src\", saddr, \"dst\", daddr,\n\t\t\t\"timestamp_ns\", ts, \"audit.violation\", violation,\n\t\t)\n\tcase auditConnect:\n\t\tslog.InfoContext(ctx, \"ebpf/audit: connect\",\n\t\t\t\"pid\", pid, \"comm\", comm, \"timestamp_ns\", ts,\n\t\t)\n\t}\n}\n\nfunc (c *Consumer) ipAllowed(ip string) bool {\n\tif len(c.allowedNets) == 0 {\n\t\treturn true\n\t}\n\tparsed := net.ParseIP(ip)\n\tif parsed == nil {\n\t\treturn false\n\t}\n\tfor _, n := range c.allowedNets {\n\t\tif n.Contains(parsed) {\n\t\t\treturn true\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\treturn false\n}\n\n// Close stops the consumer. Implements io.Closer.\nfunc (c *Consumer) Close() error {\n\tselect {\n\tcase <-c.stop:\n\tdefault:\n\t\tclose(c.stop)\n\t}\n\tif c.reader != nil {\n\t\treturn c.reader.Close()\n\t}\n\treturn nil\n}\n\nvar _ io.Closer = (*Consumer)(nil)\n\n// parseCIDRs parses a slice of CIDR strings into *net.IPNet values.\nfunc parseCIDRs(cidrs []string) ([]*net.IPNet, error) {\n\tnets := make([]*net.IPNet, 0, len(cidrs))\n\tfor _, cidr := range cidrs {\n\t\t_, n, err := net.ParseCIDR(cidr)\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"invalid CIDR %q: %w\", cidr, err)\n\t\t}\n\t\tnets = append(nets, n)\n\t}\n\treturn nets, nil\n}\n\nfunc nullTermStr(b []byte) string {\n\tfor i, c := range b {\n\t\tif c == 0 {\n\t\t\treturn string(b[:i])\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\treturn string(b)\n}\n\nfunc sliceContains(s []string, v string) bool {\n\tfor _, x := range s {\n\t\tif x == v {\n\t\t\treturn true\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\treturn false\n}\n```\n\n- [ ] **Step 6.3: Add non-Linux not defined.\n\n- [ ] **Step 8.2: Add initEBPF function**\n\nCreate `cmd/api/ebpf.go`:\n\n```go\n//go:build linux\n\npackage main\n\nimport (\n\t\"log/slog\"\n\n\t\"go.opentelemetry.io/otel\"\n\n\t\"github.com/ravencloak-org/Raven/internal/config\"\n\t\"github.com/ravencloak-org/Raven/internal/ebpf\"\n\t\"github.com/ravencloak-org/Raven/internal/ebpf/audit\"\n\t\"github.com/ravencloak-org/Raven/internal/ebpf/observability\"\n\t\"github.com/ravencloak-org/Raven/internal/ebpf/xdp\"\n)\n\n// initEBPF starts the eBPF subsystem based on cfg.\n// It always returns a non-nil Manager — features that fail to start are logged\n// and skipped; the API server continues regardless.\nfunc initEBPF(cfg *config.EBPFConfig, _ interface{}) (*ebpf.Manager, error) {\n\tmanager := ebpf.NewManager()\n\n\tif err := ebpf.CheckCapabilities(); err != nil {\n\t\tslog.Warn(\"eBPF subsystem ---

## Review: eBPF Edge Optimization Implementation Plan (M10)

### Summary

The plan is well-structured, and the spec is sound in its design principles. However, I identified several specific issues across the eight review criteria. None are architectural showstoppers, but five issues are Important (must be fixed before the PR merges) and several are Suggestions.

---

### Criteria 1 — Each task produces working, testable software on its own

**Pass with one caveat.**

Task 5 (observability collector) has a structural problem: `collector.go` (line 1007) declares `Start(ctx context.Context)` but `stub.go` (line 1075) declares `Start(_ interface{ Done() <-chan struct{} })`. These signatures are incompatible. Any code that stores a `*Collector` through a common interface will fail to compile on macOS because the stub `Start` does not match the Linux signature.

The same asymmetry exists in Task 6 (`audit/stub.go` line 1392) and Task 7 (`xdp/stub.go` — not shown in full, but the pattern is repeated). The stubs use an anonymous interface for the context argument, whereas the Linux implementations use `context.Context`. Since `context.Context` satisfies `interface{ Done() <-chan struct{} }` but the reverse is not true, callers in `main.go` that pass a real `context.Context` to the stub's `Start` will fail to compile. If an interface type is ever defined for `Collector`/`Consumer`/`Controller` (which the wiring in `cmd/api/ebpf.go` implicitly needs), this inconsistency will surface immediately.

**Fix:** Change all stub `Start` signatures to `Start(ctx context.Context)` and import `context` in each stub file.

---

### Criteria 2 — File paths consistent with existing codebase layout

**Pass.**

All new paths (`internal/ebpf/`, `internal/ebpf/observability/`, `internal/ebpf/audit/`, `internal/ebpf/xdp/`, `internal/ebpf/programs/`, `cmd/api/ebpf.go`, `cmd/api/ebpf_stub.go`) are consistent with the existing layout. The `internal/config/config.go` modification is correctly targeted. The module path `github.com/ravencloak-org/Raven` is used correctly in all new import paths.

One precision point: the plan (Task 1.3) says to add `EBPFConfig` "after the `STTConfig` struct (around line 92)". In the actual file, `STTConfig` ends at line 92. The `EBPFConfig` struct should be inserted after line 92 and `EBPF EBPFConfig` added to the `Config` struct (line 29 area) after `STT STTConfig`. The line reference is correct but the plan should clarify the Config struct field placement is also required — it does, but only implicitly. Not a blocker.

---

### Criteria 3 — Build tags consistently applied

**Important issue found.**

The `capabilities_test.go` file in Task 3A carries `//go:build linux`, which is correct. However, the test `TestManager_StopIsIdempotent` is added to the same file (Step 3B.1) but `manager.go` also carries `//go:build linux`. The `Manager` type and `NewManager` function in `stub.go` are tagged `//go:build !linux`. Since the test file is tagged `//go:build linux`, the test can only run on Linux. This is intentional and acceptable.

The more significant gap: the plan creates `internal/ebpf/stub.go` for the `!linux` path but Task 3 creates only `capabilities.go` and `manager.go` for the Linux path. There is no corresponding Linux-tagged `loader.go` or `maps.go` despite both appearing in the File Map at the top of the plan. These files are referenced in the spec but have no implementation task. This means the `internal/ebpf` package as described in the File Map is never complete as a standalone unit.

**Fix:** Either add tasks for `loader.go` and `maps.go` (even if they are stubs at this stage), or explicitly note they are deferred to a later task and remove them from the File Map to avoid confusion.

---

### Criteria 4 — cilium/ebpf API usage

**Important issue found: XDP attachment is missing from controller.go.**

The spec (spec line 149–157) describes `controller.go` as attaching the XDP program to the interface on startup using `link.AttachXDP` with `XDPOptions`. The plan's Task 7 `controller.go` (plan line 1499–1568) contains no XDP attachment code at all — `NewController` accepts an `XDPObjects` interface but never calls `link.AttachXDP`. The `SyncBlocklist` function is a stub. This is acknowledged as "wired in Task 9 after bpf2go generates typed accessors," but Task 9 only addresses the Dockerfile/Makefile — there is no task that adds the `link.AttachXDP` call.

The `XDPObjects` interface in the plan (line 1488–1492) has only `io.Closer` — it does not expose the XDP program handle needed to call `link.AttachXDP(iface, prog, &link.XDPOptions{...})`. The generated struct from bpf2go will have `objs.XdpFilter` (a `*ebpf.Program`), but nothing in the plan shows how that gets wired to `link.AttachXDP`.

**Fix:** Either add a Task 12 that wires the real bpf2go objects into the controller (showing `link.AttachXDP` usage), or make `XDPObjects` expose the program field and show how the `link.Link` returned by `AttachXDP` is stored and detached in `Close()`.

**Secondary issue:** The `go:generate` directives in Task 11 use a shell substitution inside a Go string literal:

```go
//go:generate go run github.com/cilium/ebpf/cmd/bpf2go -target amd64,arm64 Observability ../programs/observability.c -- -I/usr/include/$(shell uname -m)-linux-gnu
```

The `$(shell ...)` syntax is a Makefile construct. Inside a `//go:generate` comment, the shell processes it as a literal subshell via `sh -c`, so `$(uname -m)` is the correct form. The plan uses `$(shell uname -m)` which is Makefile syntax and will pass literally to the shell, generating an include path like `/usr/include/$(shell uname -m)-linux-gnu` rather than `/usr/include/x86_64-linux-gnu`.

**Fix:** Replace `$(shell uname -m)` with `$(uname -m)` in all three `//go:generate` directives (plan lines 1972, 1977, 1982).

---

### Criteria 5 — Graceful degradation clearly tested

**Pass with suggestion.**

The `TestEBPFManager_GracefulDegradeOnCI` test (plan line 1647–1661) asserts `manager` is not nil even when `initEBPF` returns an error. This is the correct pattern.

However, the test uses `_ = err` — it explicitly ignores the error. This means if `initEBPF` panics rather than returning an error, the test would fail with a panic rather than a clean assertion. The comment says "On CI without CAP_BPF, err may be non-nil but manager must not be nil." The test should assert `manager != nil` more explicitly and optionally log the error for diagnostics:

```go
assert.NotNil(t, manager)
if err != nil {
    t.Logf("eBPF unavailable (expected on CI): %v", err)
}
```

Not a blocker, but worth noting.

---

### Criteria 6 — Config task includes power-of-2 validation and env var bindings

**Pass with one issue.**

The power-of-2 validation in Task 1.3 (plan line 184–190) is gated on `cfg.EBPF.AuditEnabled`:

```go
if cfg.EBPF.AuditEnabled {
    size := cfg.EBPF.AuditRingBufferSize
    if size <= 0 || bits.OnesCount(uint(size)) != 1 {
        return nil, fmt.Errorf(...)
    }
}
```

The spec (spec line 119) states "A non-power-of-2 value returns a clear config error before any BPF load is attempted." This is technically satisfied, but the validation only fires when `AuditEnabled=true`. If an operator sets `RAVEN_EBPF_AUDIT_RING_BUFFER_SIZE=1000000` without enabling audit, they get no feedback that their value is invalid. When they later set `AuditEnabled=true`, the error will appear — which is acceptable but surprising.

The test `TestEBPFConfig_InvalidRingBufferSize` (plan line 110–119) sets `AuditEnabled` implicitly to `false` (the default) but expects an error. **This test will not produce an error** under the current validation logic since the gate `if cfg.EBPF.AuditEnabled` will be false. The test as written will fail even after implementation, contradicting the "Expected: PASS (3 tests)" statement.

**Fix:** Either validate unconditionally (remove the `if cfg.EBPF.AuditEnabled` guard), or update the test to also set `RAVEN_EBPF_AUDIT_ENABLED=true`. The spec intent ("returns a clear config error") suggests unconditional validation is preferred.

All env var bindings are present and correctly mapped.

---

### Criteria 7 — Docker/CI changes safe (no CAP_SYS_ADMIN, correct CGO_ENABLED)

**Pass on capabilities.** The plan correctly uses `CAP_BPF` + `CAP_NET_ADMIN` and explicitly calls out that `CAP_SYS_ADMIN` must not be added.

**Important issue on CGO_ENABLED in CI (Task 10).**

Task 10.2 changes the `build-and-test` CI job's `go build` step globally to `CGO_ENABLED=1`. This means the standard CI build now requires a C toolchain on the runner. The plan adds `sudo apt-get install -y clang llvm libbpf-dev` before this step, which mitigates the issue on Linux runners. However, this breaks the existing non-eBPF `go build ./cmd/api` pattern — the current Dockerfile uses `CGO_ENABLED=0` for a fully static binary, and the plan changes the _Docker_ builder to `CGO_ENABLED=1` permanently (not conditionally). There is no toggle to build without eBPF in the Dockerfile anymore.

The spec (spec lines 209–224) describes a split: existing `build-arm64` target stays `CGO_ENABLED=0`, and a new `build-arm64-ebpf` is added. The Makefile correctly follows this split. But the Dockerfile does not — the plan replaces `CGO_ENABLED=0` wholesale (plan line 1828). Operators who do not want eBPF cannot opt out of the CGO dependency in Docker builds.

**Fix:** Add a build arg to the Dockerfile:
```dockerfile
ARG EBPF_ENABLED=false
RUN if [ "$EBPF_ENABLED" = "true" ]; then \
      CGO_ENABLED=1 go build -ldflags="-s -w -extldflags '-static'" -o /api ./cmd/api; \
    else \
      CGO_ENABLED=0 go build -ldflags="-s -w" -o /api ./cmd/api; \
    fi
```
Or document that the Dockerfile is now always eBPF-enabled and the non-eBPF path is Makefile-only.

Additionally, the CI `go.yml` change installs `clang llvm libbpf-dev` only in the `build-and-test` job. The `lint` job (which also runs `go build` implicitly via `golangci-lint`) does not get these packages, so linting will fail for any eBPF-specific code that needs CGO. Add the same `apt-get install` step to the `lint` job.

---

### Criteria 8 — No tasks that break the macOS build

**Pass overall, with one precision issue.**

The `//go:build !linux` stubs correctly provide no-op implementations for all Linux-specific types. The pattern is sound.

One latent macOS build break: `internal/ebpf/capabilities.go` imports `github.com/cilium/ebpf/rlimit` and is tagged `//go:build linux`. The `stub.go` does not import `rlimit` and does not call `RemoveMemlock`. This is fine. However, Task 3A does not create the non-Linux stub for `rlimit` usage — it is entirely absent from the non-Linux path. Since `capabilities.go` is gated `linux`, the macOS build never sees it. Confirmed safe.

The `go:generate` directives are placed inside files tagged `//go:build linux`. On macOS, `go generate ./internal/ebpf/...` will not run these directives since the build tag excludes the files. The plan's CI step (`go generate ./internal/ebpf/...` in Task 10.2) runs on `ubuntu-latest`, which is correct. However, the plan's environment notes say "On macOS, only the Go stubs are compiled" and "generated `_bpfel.go` files are committed." This means the `go generate` step in CI serves as a staleness check, not generation, since the files must already be committed. The CI step `git diff --exit-code` is correct for this purpose.

---

### Issue Summary

| Severity | Location | Issue |
|----------|----------|-------|
| Important | Plan Task 5/6/7 stubs, lines 1075, 1392 | `Start` signature mismatch between Linux impl (`context.Context`) and stubs (anonymous interface) — breaks shared interface extraction |
| Important | Plan Task 6, config validation, lines 184–119 | `TestEBPFConfig_InvalidRingBufferSize` will not produce an error because `AuditEnabled` defaults to false; test contradicts its own expected result |
| Important | Plan Tasks 7 and 9, no Task 12 | `link.AttachXDP` with `XDPOptions` is never called anywhere in the plan; the XDP controller never actually attaches a program |
| Important | Plan Task 10.2 (lint job) | `lint` job does not install `clang llvm libbpf-dev`; golangci-lint will fail on CGO-dependent eBPF code |
| Important | Plan Task 11 lines 1972/1977/1982 | `$(shell uname -m)` is Makefile syntax and will not expand in `//go:generate`; use `$(uname -m)` |

**Verdict: Issues Found**

The plan is nearly production-ready. The five Important issues above must be resolved before implementation begins. The most critical are the `link.AttachXDP` gap (the XDP feature would be a compile-correct no-op that silently never drops a packet) and the config test logic error (a test will be green for the wrong reason). The `$(shell ...)` directive bug will silently produce wrong include paths in generated files on any architecture.

1	# eBPF Edge Optimization (M10) Implementation Plan
2	
3	> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.
4	
5	**Goal:** Implement three eBPF-based features (#120 XDP Pre-filtering, #122 Kernel Observability, #123 Security Audit Trail) on a shared Go foundation inside the Raven API binary, with full graceful degradation when eBPF is unavailable.
6	
7	**Architecture:** A single `internal/ebpf/` package owns the eBPF lifecycle (capabilities check, loader, manager). Three sub-packages (`observability/`, `audit/`, `xdp/`) each own one feature. BPF C programs live in `internal/ebpf/programs/` and are compiled by `bpf2go` at build time — the bytecode is embedded into the Go binary, so edge nodes need no C toolchain at runtime. All features are opt-in via env vars and self-disable with a structured `slog` warning if the kernel, capabilities, or BTF are missing.
8	
9	**Tech Stack:** Go 1.26 + `github.com/cilium/ebpf` v0.17+ (bpf2go, link, ringbuf, rlimit), `go.opentelemetry.io/otel/metric` (existing), clang/llvm (build-time only), Linux kernel ≥ 5.8 with BTF.
10	
11	**Implementation order (lowest risk first):**
12	1. Config (`internal/config/config.go`)
13	2. Shared foundation (`internal/ebpf/`)
14	3. Feature #122 Kernel Observability (`internal/ebpf/observability/`)
15	4. Feature #123 Security Audit Trail (`internal/ebpf/audit/`)
16	5. Feature #120 XDP Pre-filtering (`internal/ebpf/xdp/`)
17	6. Wire into `cmd/api/main.go`
18	7. Build pipeline (`Dockerfile`, `Makefile.edge`)
19	8. CI updates (`.github/workflows/go.yml`)
20	
21	---
22

24

28	| `internal/ebpf/capabilities.go` | CAP_BPF / CAP_NET_ADMIN check, kernel version floor (≥5.8), `rlimit.RemoveMemlock()` |
29	| `internal/ebpf/loader.go` | Thin wrapper: detect BTF, call `loadXxxObjects`, return typed error |
30	| `internal/ebpf/manager.go` | `Manager` struct: `Start()` / `Stop()` / `io.Closer`; detaches all probes |
31	| `internal/ebpf/maps.go` | Typed BPF map helpers shared between features |
32	| `internal/ebpf/programs/observability.c` | BPF C: sched_switch, raw_syscalls/sys_exit, net tracepoints, __fd_install kprobe |
33	| `internal/ebpf/programs/audit.c` | BPF C: execve tracepoint, inet_sock_set_state, sys_enter_connect |
34	| `internal/ebpf/programs/xdp.c` | BPF C: XDP hook, LPM trie lookup, throttle |
35	| `internal/ebpf/observability/collector.go` | Polls BPF maps → OTel Gauge/Counter instruments |
36	| `internal/ebpf/observability/collector_test.go` | Unit tests with mock BPF maps |
37	| `internal/ebpf/audit/consumer.go` | Ring buffer reader goroutine → slog JSON events, allowlist checks |

{
  "replace_all": false,
